I don't feel like I *have* to do it to fix something in my head, my body just starts doing it, or stops doing anything at all.\n\nDoctor: Do these actions happen without you being aware of them, or do you realize you're doing them while it's happening?\n\nPatient: I... I usually realize it while it's happening. It's not like I black out or forget where I am. I'm just standing there, and I know I'm tapping the scanner or just frozen, but I can't really... snap out of it right away. I see the customer looking at me, I know they're waiting, but my body just stays stuck in whatever it's doing.\n\nDoctor: Before these episodes happen, do you ever feel a strange sensation or an urge in your body that makes you feel like something needs to be done or felt just right?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel anything like that. There's no... no build-up or urge in my body beforehand. I don't feel like things need to be just right, or anything like that. It just happens on its own, without any warning or feeling leading up to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed these kinds of episodes happening anywhere other than work, like when you're at home or doing other activities?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I... I haven't noticed it happening at home or anywhere else. It seems to mostly just be when I'm at work. When I'm at home, I'm usually just by myself, so... I don't think it happens there. At least, I haven't caught myself doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed any other times when your body moves on its own without you meaning to, like sudden jerks, twitches, or other movements, even if they're small?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. No jerks or twitches... just the freezing up or the tapping at work. My body doesn't really move on its own otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced or witnessed any traumatic or highly stressful events around the time these episodes at work started happening?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I mean... work can get a little busy sometimes, just being on the register, but I haven't been through anything traumatic. There hasn't been any major stress or big events lately. It's really just been the usual day-to-day routine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had episodes like this before, even if just once or twice, or is this something that's only started recently?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't had anything like this happen before. It's... it's just been recently, over the last little while. Before this started at work, everything was pretty normal. So, no, nothing like this in the past.\n\nDoctor: While you're having one of these episodes at work, are you fully aware of what's going on around you, or do you feel spaced out or confused during it?\n\nPatient: No, I'm fully aware. I... I hear the customers, I know I'm standing at the register. I don't feel confused or spaced out at all. I just get stuck, or I'm doing that tapping thing. But my mind is right there, I know exactly what's happening around me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I just keep getting stuck doing things at the register that don't really fit the moment. \n\nLike, I'll start tapping the scanner in a certain pattern, or I'll just freeze completely with my hand hovering right over the cash drawer while the customer is standing there waiting on me. It's been happening a lot, and, well, last week it actually got me written up. So I figured I needed to come see someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repeated thoughts that you can't control, or do you feel like you have to perform certain actions in a specific way to reduce anxiety?\n\nPatient: No, it's not really like that. I don't... I don't have thoughts telling me to do it or anything. And I wouldn't say I feel anxious before it happens, either. It's just... it just happens. I don't feel like I *have* to do it to fix something in my head, my body just starts doing it, or stops doing anything at all.\n\nDoctor: Do these actions happen without you being aware of them, or do you realize you're doing them while it's happening?\n\nPatient: I... I usually realize it while it's happening. It's not like I black out or forget where I am. I'm just standing there, and I know I'm tapping the scanner or just frozen, but I can't really... snap out of it right away. I see the customer looking at me, I know they're waiting, but my body just stays stuck in whatever it's doing.\n\nDoctor: Before these episodes happen, do you ever feel a strange sensation or an urge in your body that makes you feel like something needs to be done or felt just right?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel anything like that. There's no... no build-up or urge in my body beforehand. I don't feel like things need to be just right, or anything like that. It just happens on its own, without any warning or feeling leading up to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed these kinds of episodes happening anywhere other than work, like when you're at home or doing other activities?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I... I haven't noticed it happening at home or anywhere else. It seems to mostly just be when I'm at work. When I'm at home, I'm usually just by myself, so... I don't think it happens there. At least, I haven't caught myself doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed any other times when your body moves on its own without you meaning to, like sudden jerks, twitches, or other movements, even if they're small?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. Not even once or twice. I've never dealt with anything like this in the past until this all started recently.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone in your family ever been diagnosed with a neurological condition or a movement disorder, like Parkinson’s disease, Huntington’s disease, or something similar?\n\nPatient: No, not that I know of. Nobody in my family has ever had Parkinson's or Huntington's, or anything like that. It just happens on its own, without any warning or feeling leading up to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed these kinds of episodes happening anywhere other than work, like when you're at home or doing other activities?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I... I haven't noticed it happening at home or anywhere else. It seems to mostly just be when I'm at work. When I'm at home, I'm usually just by myself, so... I don't think it happens there. At least, I haven't caught myself doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed any other times when your body moves on its own without you meaning to, like sudden jerks, twitches, or other movements, even if they're small?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. No jerks or twitches... just the freezing up or the tapping at work. My body doesn't really move on its own otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced or witnessed any traumatic or highly stressful events around the time these episodes at work started happening?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I mean... work can get a little busy sometimes, just being on the register, but I haven't been through anything traumatic. There hasn't been any major stress or big events lately. It's really just been the usual day-to-day routine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had episodes like this before, even if just once or twice, or is this something that's only started recently?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't had anything like this happen before. It's... it's just been recently, over the last little while. Before this started at work, everything was pretty normal. So, no, nothing like this in the past.\n\nDoctor: While you're having one of these episodes at work, are you fully aware of what's going on around you, or do you feel spaced out or confused during it?\n\nPatient: No, I'm fully aware. I... I hear the customers, I know I'm standing at the register. I don't feel confused or spaced out at all. I just get stuck, or I'm doing that tapping thing. But my mind is right there, I know exactly what's happening around me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had anything like these episodes happen to you before, even just once or twice, even if it didn't happen repeatedly like it is now?\n\nPatient: No, like I mentioned just a minute ago, I really haven't. Not even once or twice. I've never dealt with anything like this in the past until this all started recently.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone in your family ever been diagnosed with a neurological condition or a movement disorder, like Parkinson’s disease, Huntington’s disease, or something similar?\n\nPatient: No, not that I know of. As far as I know, there's no history of any neurological issues or movement problems with my parents or anyone else in the family.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been diagnosed with any psychiatric or developmental conditions, like anxiety, depression, autism, or obsessive-compulsive disorder?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I've never been diagnosed with anxiety or depression, or anything like OCD or autism. I see the customer looking at me, I know they're waiting, but my body just stays stuck in whatever it's doing.\n\nDoctor: Before these episodes happen, do you ever feel a strange sensation or an urge in your body that makes you feel like something needs to be done or felt just right?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel anything like that. There's no... no build-up or urge in my body beforehand. I don't feel like things need to be just right, or anything like that. It just happens on its own, without any warning or feeling leading up to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed these kinds of episodes happening anywhere other than work, like when you're at home or doing other activities?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I... I haven't noticed it happening at home or anywhere else. It seems to mostly just be when I'm at work. When I'm at home, I'm usually just by myself, so... I don't think it happens there. At least, I haven't caught myself doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed any other times when your body moves on its own without you meaning to, like sudden jerks, twitches, or other movements, even if they're small?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. No jerks or twitches... just the freezing up or the tapping at work. My body doesn't really move on its own otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced or witnessed any traumatic or highly stressful events around the time these episodes at work started happening?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I mean... work can get a little busy sometimes, just being on the register, but I haven't been through anything traumatic. There hasn't been any major stress or big events lately. It's really just been the usual day-to-day routine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had episodes like this before, even if just once or twice, or is this something that's only started recently?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't had anything like this happen before. It's... it's just been recently, over the last little while. Before this started at work, everything was pretty normal. So, no, nothing like this in the past.\n\nDoctor: While you're having one of these episodes at work, are you fully aware of what's going on around you, or do you feel spaced out or confused during it?\n\nPatient: No, I'm fully aware. I... I hear the customers, I know I'm standing at the register. I don't feel confused or spaced out at all. I just get stuck, or I'm doing that tapping thing. But my mind is right there, I know exactly what's happening around me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had anything like these episodes happen to you before, even just once or twice, even if it didn't happen repeatedly like it is now?\n\nPatient: No, like I mentioned just a minute ago, I really haven't. Not even once or twice. I've never dealt with anything like this in the past until this all started recently.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone in your family ever been diagnosed with a neurological condition or a movement disorder, like Parkinson’s disease, Huntington’s disease, or something similar?\n\nPatient: No, not that I know of. Nobody in my family has ever had Parkinson's or Huntington's, or anything like that. As far as I know, there's no history of any neurological issues or movement problems with my parents or anyone else in the family.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been diagnosed with any psychiatric or developmental conditions, like anxiety, depression, autism, or obsessive-compulsive disorder?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I've never been diagnosed with anxiety or depression, or anything like OCD or autism. I've never really had any mental health issues or seen a psychiatrist before now.\n\nDoctor: While you're having one of these episodes, do you ever feel like you're losing control of yourself, or like part of you is watching what's happening without being able to take over?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't have any weird blinking or lip movements. And my fingers... well, aside from the tapping I already told you about, they don't really twitch on their own or anything. So, no, I haven't seen any of those little movements.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any episodes where you temporarily couldn't move your body, especially when falling asleep or just waking up?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that.
